+import Mathlib.Computability.Language
+import Mathlib.Data.Finset.Basic
+import Mathlib.Logic.Equiv.Defs
+
+/-!
+# Bounded-width branching programs
+
+The general branching-program model of [Bar89, §1]: an instruction reads one input bit and
+produces a map `[w] → [w]`; a width-`w` program is a finite sequence of instructions, and it
+*yields* on an input the composition of the maps its instructions produce.  A family of programs
+recognizes a language through accepting sets `F_n` of maps.  Permutation programs (every map a
+bijection) are the predicate `IsPermutation`.
+
+## Main definitions
+
+* `BranchingProgram.Instr` — an instruction `⟨var, map0, map1⟩` (BV14's order: `map0` when the
+  bit is `0`), with `Instr.eval`, `Instr.IsPermutation` and `Instr.ofPerm`.
+* `BranchingProgram` — a width-`w` program on `n` inputs (registered model); `length`, `append`
+  (the `++` notation), `yield`, `IsPermutation`, `conj` (Lemma 1's conjugation) and
+  `complementLast` (Lemma 2's change of the last instruction).
+* `BranchingProgramFamily` — a family with accepting sets (registered model); `language`,
+  `IsPolyLength`, `IsPermutation`.
+* `Language.InBWBP`, `Language.InPBP` — the classes BWBP and `PBP_w` (polynomial length).
+
+## Main results
+
+All are statements of the 6a statement phase (targets admitted with `sorry`), except the readers.
+
+* `BranchingProgram.yield_append`, `yield_nil` — the yield composes in program order (C1).
+* `BranchingProgram.yield_conj`, `length_conj`, `length_append` — conjugation (C2).
+* `BranchingProgram.yield_complementLast`, `length_complementLast` — Lemma 2's step (C3).
+* `BranchingProgram.isPermutation_iff` (proved) and the closure lemmas of permutation
+  programs, `IsPermutation.bijective_yield` (C4).
+
+## Conventions and deviations
+
+* **Instruction order.** [Bar89, §1] writes `⟨j, f, g⟩` with `f` used when `x_j = 1`; the fields
+  here follow [BV14, §3.3]'s `(var(t), σ_{t,0}, σ_{t,1})`: `map0` is used when the bit is `0`.
+* **Program order.** [Bar89] writes products left to right (the leftmost map acts first).  The
+  model composes in program order: `(B₁ ++ B₂).yield x = B₂.yield x ∘ B₁.yield x`.  In Mathlib's
+  `Equiv.Perm` product (right factor first) Bar89's word `σ₁σ₂σ₁⁻¹σ₂⁻¹` is
+  `σ₂⁻¹ * σ₁⁻¹ * σ₂ * σ₁`.
+* **Recognition** is by accepting sets, as in [Bar89, §1]: `x ∈ L ↔ B(x) ∈ F_n`.
+
+## References
+
+* [Bar89] D. A. Mix Barrington, *Bounded-width polynomial-size branching programs recognize
+  exactly those languages in NC¹*, J. Comput. System Sci. 38(1):150–164, 1989.
+* [BV14] Z. Brakerski, V. Vaikuntanathan, *Lattice-based FHE as secure as PKE*, ITCS 2014,
+  pp. 1–12; IACR ePrint 2013/541.
+-/
+
+set_option maxHeartbeats 0
+set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
+set_option autoImplicit false
+
+/-- An instruction `⟨var, map0, map1⟩` of width `w` over `n` inputs: read input `var`; apply
+`map0 : [w] → [w]` if it is `0` and `map1` if it is `1` [Bar89, §1, p001-b002].
+Deviation: Bar89 lists the `x_j = 1` map first; the fields follow BV14's
+`(var(t), σ_{t,0}, σ_{t,1})` [BV14, §3.3, p013-b002]. -/
+structure BranchingProgram.Instr (w n : ℕ) where
+  /-- The input bit the instruction reads. -/
+  var : Fin n
+  /-- The map produced when the bit is `0`. -/
+  map0 : Fin w → Fin w
+  /-- The map produced when the bit is `1`. -/
+  map1 : Fin w → Fin w
+
+/-- A width-`w` branching program on `n` inputs: a finite sequence of instructions
+`instrs = [ι₁, …, ι_l]` [Bar89, §1, p001-b002].  Registered model
+(`TCSlib/ComputationalModels.lean`). -/
+structure BranchingProgram (w n : ℕ) where
+  /-- The instructions, in program order. -/
+  instrs : List (BranchingProgram.Instr w n)
+
+namespace BranchingProgram
+
+variable {w n : ℕ}
+
+/-! ### Instructions -/
+
+/-- The map an instruction produces on input `x`: `ι.eval x = if x ι.var then ι.map1 else ι.map0`
+[Bar89, §1, p001-b002: "evaluate to `f` if `x_j = 1`, otherwise evaluate to `g`"; BV14 order]. -/
+def Instr.eval (ι : Instr w n) (x : Fin n → Bool) : Fin w → Fin w :=
+  if x ι.var then ι.map1 else ι.map0
+
+/-- Reader of `Instr.eval`: `ι.eval x = if x ι.var then ι.map1 else ι.map0`. -/
+theorem Instr.eval_eq (ι : Instr w n) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
+    ι.eval x = if x ι.var then ι.map1 else ι.map0 := rfl
+
+/-- `ι` is a permutation instruction: both of its maps are bijections,
+`Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1` [Bar89, §1, p002-b001]. -/
+def Instr.IsPermutation (ι : Instr w n) : Prop :=
+  Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1
+
+/-- Reader of `Instr.IsPermutation`:
+`ι.IsPermutation ↔ Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1`. -/
+theorem Instr.isPermutation_iff (ι : Instr w n) :
+    ι.IsPermutation ↔ Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1 := Iff.rfl
+
+/-- The instruction `⟨i, σ₀, σ₁⟩` with permutation maps: `Instr.ofPerm i σ₀ σ₁` reads input `i`
+and produces `σ₀` on `0` and `σ₁` on `1` [Bar89, §1, p002-b001; BV14, §3.3, p013-b002].  Its
+field readers are `Instr.ofPerm_var`, `Instr.ofPerm_map0`, `Instr.ofPerm_map1`. -/
+@[simps (config := { isSimp := false })]
+def Instr.ofPerm (i : Fin n) (σ₀ σ₁ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) : Instr w n where
+  var := i
+  map0 := σ₀
+  map1 := σ₁
+
+/-- An instruction built from permutations is a permutation instruction:
+`(Instr.ofPerm i σ₀ σ₁).IsPermutation`.  Glue. -/
+theorem Instr.isPermutation_ofPerm (i : Fin n) (σ₀ σ₁ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
+    (Instr.ofPerm i σ₀ σ₁ : Instr w n).IsPermutation :=
+  ⟨σ₀.bijective, σ₁.bijective⟩
+
+/-! ### Programs -/
+
+/-- The length of a program, its number of instructions: `B.length = B.instrs.length`
+[Bar89, §1, p001-b002]. -/
+def length (B : BranchingProgram w n) : ℕ :=
+  B.instrs.length
+
+/-- Reader of `length`: `B.length = B.instrs.length`. -/
+theorem length_eq (B : BranchingProgram w n) : B.length = B.instrs.length := rfl
+
+/-- Concatenation of programs, `B₁.append B₂ = ⟨B₁.instrs ++ B₂.instrs⟩`: run `B₁`, then `B₂`
+(Bar89's juxtaposition `B₁B₂`, [Bar89, Thm 1 proof, p004-b002]).  It is the `++` notation. -/
+def append (B₁ B₂ : BranchingProgram w n) : BranchingProgram w n :=
+  ⟨B₁.instrs ++ B₂.instrs⟩
+
+instance : Append (BranchingProgram w n) := ⟨append⟩
+
+/-- Reader of `append`: `B₁.append B₂ = ⟨B₁.instrs ++ B₂.instrs⟩`. -/
+theorem append_eq (B₁ B₂ : BranchingProgram w n) :
+    B₁.append B₂ = ⟨B₁.instrs ++ B₂.instrs⟩ := rfl
+
+/-- Reader of the `++` notation: `(B₁ ++ B₂).instrs = B₁.instrs ++ B₂.instrs`. -/
+theorem append_instrs (B₁ B₂ : BranchingProgram w n) :
+    (B₁ ++ B₂).instrs = B₁.instrs ++ B₂.instrs := rfl
+
+/-- The map `B` yields on `x`: the composition, in program order, of the maps its instructions
+produce, `B.yield x = ι_l.eval x ∘ ⋯ ∘ ι₁.eval x`, computed as the left fold
+`B.instrs.foldl (fun f ι => ι.eval x ∘ f) id`; `yield ⟨[]⟩ x = id` [Bar89, §1, p001-b002:
+"the composition of the functions produced by each of the instructions"].
+Deviation: Bar89's products read left to right; here the first instruction acts first. -/
+def yield (B : BranchingProgram w n) (x : Fin n → Bool) : Fin w → Fin w :=
+  B.instrs.foldl (fun f ι => ι.eval x ∘ f) id
+
+/-- Reader of `yield`: `B.yield x = B.instrs.foldl (fun f ι => ι.eval x ∘ f) id`. -/
+theorem yield_eq (B : BranchingProgram w n) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
+    B.yield x = B.instrs.foldl (fun f ι => ι.eval x ∘ f) id := rfl
+
+/-- `B` is a permutation branching program: every map of every instruction is a bijection,
+`∀ ι ∈ B.instrs, Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1`
+[Bar89, §1, p002-b001]. -/
+def IsPermutation (B : BranchingProgram w n) : Prop :=
+  ∀ ι ∈ B.instrs, Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1
+
+/-- Conjugation of a program by `θ`: every map `f` of every instruction becomes `θ ∘ f ∘ θ⁻¹`,
+`B.conj θ = ⟨B.instrs.map fun ι => ⟨ι.var, θ ∘ ι.map0 ∘ θ.symm, θ ∘ ι.map1 ∘ θ.symm⟩⟩`
+[Bar89, Lemma 1 proof, p003-b003: "replacing each `σ_i` and `τ_i` by `θσ_iθ⁻¹` and
+`θτ_iθ⁻¹`"]. -/
+def conj (B : BranchingProgram w n) (θ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) : BranchingProgram w n :=
+  ⟨B.instrs.map fun ι => ⟨ι.var, θ ∘ ι.map0 ∘ θ.symm, θ ∘ ι.map1 ∘ θ.symm⟩⟩
+
+/-- Reader of `conj`:
+`B.conj θ = ⟨B.instrs.map fun ι => ⟨ι.var, θ ∘ ι.map0 ∘ θ.symm, θ ∘ ι.map1 ∘ θ.symm⟩⟩`. -/
+theorem conj_eq (B : BranchingProgram w n) (θ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
+    B.conj θ = ⟨B.instrs.map fun ι => ⟨ι.var, θ ∘ ι.map0 ∘ θ.symm, θ ∘ ι.map1 ∘ θ.symm⟩⟩ :=
+  rfl
+
+/-- Lemma 2's change of the last instruction: the last instruction `⟨i, μ, ν⟩` becomes
+`⟨i, σ⁻¹ ∘ μ, σ⁻¹ ∘ ν⟩`, every other instruction is kept, and the empty program is unchanged:
+`B.complementLast σ = ⟨B.instrs.dropLast ++ [⟨ι.var, σ.symm ∘ ι.map0, σ.symm ∘ ι.map1⟩]⟩` when
+`ι` is the last instruction [Bar89, Lemma 2 proof, p003-b004: "`⟨i, μσ⁻¹, νσ⁻¹⟩`"].
+Deviation: Bar89's `μσ⁻¹` is read left to right (first `μ`, then `σ⁻¹`), so it is the map
+`σ⁻¹ ∘ μ` here; the definition is total (Bar89 assumes a last instruction). -/
+def complementLast (B : BranchingProgram w n) (σ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
+    BranchingProgram w n :=
+  match B.instrs.getLast? with
+  | none => B
+  | some ι => ⟨B.instrs.dropLast ++ [⟨ι.var, σ.symm ∘ ι.map0, σ.symm ∘ ι.map1⟩]⟩
+
+/-- Reader of `complementLast`, by cases on the last instruction:
+`B.complementLast σ = match B.instrs.getLast? with | none => B | some ι => ⟨B.instrs.dropLast ++
+[⟨ι.var, σ.symm ∘ ι.map0, σ.symm ∘ ι.map1⟩]⟩`. -/
+theorem complementLast_eq (B : BranchingProgram w n) (σ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
+    B.complementLast σ = match B.instrs.getLast? with
+      | none => B
+      | some ι => ⟨B.instrs.dropLast ++ [⟨ι.var, σ.symm ∘ ι.map0, σ.symm ∘ ι.map1⟩]⟩ := rfl
+
+/-- `complementLast` on the empty program: `(⟨[]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).complementLast σ =
+⟨[]⟩`. -/
+theorem complementLast_nil (σ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
+    (⟨[]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).complementLast σ = ⟨[]⟩ := rfl
+
+/-- `complementLast` on a program with last instruction `ι`:
+`(⟨l ++ [ι]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).complementLast σ =
+⟨l ++ [⟨ι.var, σ.symm ∘ ι.map0, σ.symm ∘ ι.map1⟩]⟩`.  With `complementLast_nil` it covers every
+program (`List.eq_nil_or_concat`). -/
+theorem complementLast_concat (l : List (Instr w n)) (ι : Instr w n) (σ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
+    (⟨l ++ [ι]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).complementLast σ =
+      ⟨l ++ [⟨ι.var, σ.symm ∘ ι.map0, σ.symm ∘ ι.map1⟩]⟩ := by
+  rw [complementLast_eq]
+  simp only [List.getLast?_concat, List.dropLast_concat]
+
+/-! ### Targets C1–C4 -/
+
+/-- **C1.** The yield of a concatenation composes in program order:
+`(B₁ ++ B₂).yield x = B₂.yield x ∘ B₁.yield x` [Bar89, §1, p001-b002: "the composition of the
+functions produced by each of the instructions"].
+Deviation: Bar89 writes products left to right; in Mathlib's `Equiv.Perm` product (right factor
+first) the yield of `B₁B₂` on permutations is `B₂(x) * B₁(x)` (r3 §H3.6).
+
+**Proof sketch.** Rewrite with `append_instrs` and `yield_eq`; `List.foldl_append` turns the left
+side into a fold over `B₂.instrs` started at `B₁.yield x`.  An auxiliary induction on a list `l`
+shows `l.foldl (fun f ι => ι.eval x ∘ f) g = l.foldl (fun f ι => ι.eval x ∘ f) id ∘ g`
+(composition is associative, `Function.comp_assoc`), which with `g = B₁.yield x` closes the goal. -/
+theorem yield_append (B₁ B₂ : BranchingProgram w n) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
+    (B₁ ++ B₂).yield x = B₂.yield x ∘ B₁.yield x := by
+  rw [yield_eq, yield_eq B₂, yield_eq B₁, append_instrs, List.foldl_append]
+  -- the auxiliary induction of the sketch is core's `List.foldl_hom` for `f ↦ f ∘ B₁.yield x`
+  exact List.foldl_hom (fun f => f ∘ B₁.instrs.foldl (fun f ι => ι.eval x ∘ f) id) (init := id)
+    (g₁ := fun f (ι : Instr w n) => ι.eval x ∘ f) (g₂ := fun f (ι : Instr w n) => ι.eval x ∘ f)
+    (fun _ _ => rfl)
+
+/-- **C1.** The empty program yields the identity: `(⟨[]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).yield x = id`
+[Bar89, §1, p001-b002].
+
+**Proof sketch.** `yield_eq` and `List.foldl_nil`. -/
+theorem yield_nil (x : Fin n → Bool) : (⟨[]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).yield x = id := by
+  rw [yield_eq, List.foldl_nil]
+
+/-- **C2.** Conjugating every map by `θ` conjugates the yield:
+`(B.conj θ).yield x = θ ∘ B.yield x ∘ θ.symm` [Bar89, Lemma 1 proof, p003-b003].
+
+**Proof sketch.** Rewrite with `conj_eq` and `yield_eq` and induct on `B.instrs` with the fold
+generalized to an arbitrary start map `g` (the claim becomes `foldl … (θ ∘ g ∘ θ.symm) =
+θ ∘ foldl … g ∘ θ.symm`).  The step uses `Instr.eval_eq`, splits on `x ι.var`, and cancels the
+inner `θ.symm ∘ θ` by `Equiv.symm_comp_self`. -/
+theorem yield_conj (B : BranchingProgram w n) (θ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
+    (B.conj θ).yield x = θ ∘ B.yield x ∘ θ.symm := by
+  rw [conj_eq, yield_eq, yield_eq]
+  -- the induction of the sketch is core's `List.foldl_hom` for `g ↦ θ ∘ g ∘ θ.symm`; in each
+  -- step the conjugated instruction's map is `θ ∘ ι.eval x ∘ θ.symm` (`Instr.eval_eq` and core's
+  -- `apply_ite`), and the inner `θ.symm ∘ θ` cancels
+  have h := List.foldl_hom (fun g : Fin w → Fin w => θ ∘ g ∘ θ.symm) (l := B.instrs) (init := id)
+    (g₁ := fun f ι => ι.eval x ∘ f)
+    (g₂ := fun f ι => (⟨ι.var, θ ∘ ι.map0 ∘ θ.symm, θ ∘ ι.map1 ∘ θ.symm⟩ : Instr w n).eval x ∘ f)
+    (fun g ι => by
+      have hc := apply_ite (fun f : Fin w → Fin w => θ ∘ f ∘ θ.symm) (x ι.var = true) ι.map1
+        ι.map0
+      dsimp only at hc ⊢
+      rw [Instr.eval_eq, Instr.eval_eq]
+      dsimp only
+      rw [← hc]
+      funext y
+      simp only [Function.comp_apply, Equiv.symm_apply_apply])
+  dsimp only at h
+  rw [Function.id_comp, Equiv.self_comp_symm] at h
+  rw [← h]
+  exact List.foldl_map
+
+/-- **C2.** Conjugation keeps the length: `(B.conj θ).length = B.length`
+[Bar89, Lemma 1, p003-b003: "of the same length as `B`"].
+
+**Proof sketch.** `length_eq`, `conj_eq` and `List.length_map`. -/
+theorem length_conj (B : BranchingProgram w n) (θ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
+    (B.conj θ).length = B.length := by
+  simp only [length_eq, conj_eq, List.length_map]
+
+/-- **C2.** Lengths add under concatenation: `(B₁ ++ B₂).length = B₁.length + B₂.length`
+[Bar89, Thm 1 proof, p004-b002].
+
+**Proof sketch.** `length_eq`, `append_instrs` and `List.length_append`. -/
+theorem length_append (B₁ B₂ : BranchingProgram w n) :
+    (B₁ ++ B₂).length = B₁.length + B₂.length := by
+  simp only [length_eq, append_instrs, List.length_append]
+
+/-- **C3.** Lemma 2's step post-composes the yield with `σ⁻¹`: for a nonempty program,
+`(B.complementLast σ).yield x = σ.symm ∘ B.yield x` [Bar89, Lemma 2 proof, p003-b004:
+"`B'(x) = e` if `x ∈ A` and `B'(x) = σ⁻¹` if `x ∉ A`"].
+Deviation: Bar89's `B(x)σ⁻¹` is read left to right; in program order it is `σ⁻¹ ∘ B(x)`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Write `B.instrs = l ++ [ι]` (`List.eq_nil_or_concat`; the empty case contradicts
+`hB` through `length_eq`).  Then `complementLast_concat`, `yield_eq` and `List.foldl_append`
+reduce both sides to the last step: the new last map is `σ.symm ∘ ι.eval x` by `Instr.eval_eq`
+(split on `x ι.var`), and composition is associative. -/
+theorem yield_complementLast (B : BranchingProgram w n) (σ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w))
+    (hB : 0 < B.length) (x : Fin n → Bool) :
+    (B.complementLast σ).yield x = σ.symm ∘ B.yield x := by
+  obtain ⟨instrs⟩ := B
+  rcases List.eq_nil_or_concat' instrs with rfl | ⟨l, ι, rfl⟩
+  · exact absurd hB (Nat.lt_irrefl 0)
+  · -- the last step: the new last map is `σ.symm ∘ ι.eval x`
+    rw [complementLast_concat, yield_eq, yield_eq]
+    simp only [List.foldl_append, List.foldl_cons, List.foldl_nil]
+    rw [Instr.eval_eq, Instr.eval_eq]
+    dsimp only
+    cases x ι.var <;> rfl
+
+/-- **C3.** Lemma 2's step keeps the length: `(B.complementLast σ).length = B.length`
+[Bar89, Lemma 2, p003-b004: "in length `l`"].
+
+**Proof sketch.** Cases by `List.eq_nil_or_concat` on `B.instrs`: `complementLast_nil` is the
+identity, and `complementLast_concat` replaces one instruction (`List.length_append`). -/
+theorem length_complementLast (B : BranchingProgram w n) (σ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) :
+    (B.complementLast σ).length = B.length := by
+  obtain ⟨instrs⟩ := B
+  rcases List.eq_nil_or_concat' instrs with rfl | ⟨l, ι, rfl⟩
+  · rw [complementLast_nil]
+  · simp only [complementLast_concat, length_eq, List.length_append, List.length_singleton]
+
+/-- **C4.** Reader of `IsPermutation` [Bar89, §1, p002-b001]:
+`B.IsPermutation ↔ ∀ ι ∈ B.instrs, Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1`.
+It is the definition's reader and is proved (`Iff.rfl`). -/
+theorem isPermutation_iff (B : BranchingProgram w n) :
+    B.IsPermutation ↔ ∀ ι ∈ B.instrs, Function.Bijective ι.map0 ∧ Function.Bijective ι.map1 :=
+  Iff.rfl
+
+/-- **C4.** Permutation programs are closed under concatenation: if `B₁` and `B₂` are permutation
+programs, so is `B₁ ++ B₂`.  Glue for [Bar89, Thm 1 proof, p004-b002].
+
+**Proof sketch.** `isPermutation_iff`, `append_instrs` and `List.mem_append`. -/
+theorem IsPermutation.append {B₁ B₂ : BranchingProgram w n} (h₁ : B₁.IsPermutation)
+    (h₂ : B₂.IsPermutation) : (B₁ ++ B₂).IsPermutation := by
+  rw [isPermutation_iff] at h₁ h₂ ⊢
+  intro ι hι
+  rw [append_instrs, List.mem_append] at hι
+  exact hι.elim (h₁ ι) (h₂ ι)
+
+/-- **C4.** Conjugation keeps a permutation program a permutation program:
+`B.IsPermutation → (B.conj θ).IsPermutation`.  Glue for [Bar89, Lemma 1 proof, p003-b003].
+
+**Proof sketch.** `isPermutation_iff`, `conj_eq`, `List.mem_map`; each new map `θ ∘ f ∘ θ.symm` is
+a composition of bijections (`Function.Bijective.comp`, `Equiv.bijective`). -/
+theorem IsPermutation.conj {B : BranchingProgram w n} (h : B.IsPermutation)
+    (θ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) : (B.conj θ).IsPermutation := by
+  rw [isPermutation_iff] at h ⊢
+  rw [conj_eq]
+  intro ι hι
+  simp only [List.mem_map] at hι
+  obtain ⟨ι₀, hι₀, rfl⟩ := hι
+  exact ⟨θ.bijective.comp ((h ι₀ hι₀).1.comp θ.symm.bijective),
+    θ.bijective.comp ((h ι₀ hι₀).2.comp θ.symm.bijective)⟩
+
+/-- **C4.** Lemma 2's step keeps a permutation program a permutation program:
+`B.IsPermutation → (B.complementLast σ).IsPermutation`.  Glue for
+[Bar89, Lemma 2 proof, p003-b004].
+
+**Proof sketch.** Cases by `List.eq_nil_or_concat`; with `complementLast_concat` the instructions of
+`l` are unchanged and the new last maps `σ.symm ∘ f` are compositions of bijections
+(`Function.Bijective.comp`). -/
+theorem IsPermutation.complementLast {B : BranchingProgram w n} (h : B.IsPermutation)
+    (σ : Equiv.Perm (Fin w)) : (B.complementLast σ).IsPermutation := by
+  obtain ⟨instrs⟩ := B
+  rcases List.eq_nil_or_concat' instrs with rfl | ⟨l, ι, rfl⟩
+  · rw [complementLast_nil]
+    exact h
+  · rw [isPermutation_iff] at h
+    rw [complementLast_concat, isPermutation_iff]
+    intro ι' hι'
+    rcases List.mem_append.1 hι' with hl | hl
+    · -- an unchanged instruction of `l`
+      exact h ι' (List.mem_append_left _ hl)
+    · -- the new last instruction `⟨ι.var, σ.symm ∘ ι.map0, σ.symm ∘ ι.map1⟩`
+      rw [List.mem_singleton] at hl
+      subst hl
+      have hι := h ι (List.mem_append_right _ (List.mem_singleton.2 rfl))
+      exact ⟨σ.symm.bijective.comp hι.1, σ.symm.bijective.comp hι.2⟩
+
+/-- **C4.** A permutation program yields a bijection on every input:
+`B.IsPermutation → Function.Bijective (B.yield x)`.  Glue for [Bar89, §1, p002-b001].
+
+**Proof sketch.** Induction on `B.instrs` with the fold generalized to a bijective start map:
+`yield_eq`, and at each step `Instr.eval_eq` picks `map0` or `map1`, bijective by
+`isPermutation_iff`, so the composite stays bijective (`Function.Bijective.comp`). -/
+theorem IsPermutation.bijective_yield {B : BranchingProgram w n} (h : B.IsPermutation)
+    (x : Fin n → Bool) : Function.Bijective (B.yield x) := by
+  rw [isPermutation_iff] at h
+  rw [yield_eq]
+  -- the induction of the sketch is core's `List.foldlRecOn` with motive `Function.Bijective`
+  -- each step: `Instr.eval_eq` picks `map1` or `map0`, both bijective
+  refine List.foldlRecOn (motive := Function.Bijective) B.instrs _ Function.bijective_id
+    fun g hg ι hι => ?_
+  dsimp only
+  rw [Instr.eval_eq]
+  split
+  · exact (h ι hι).2.comp hg
+  · exact (h ι hι).1.comp hg
+
+end BranchingProgram
+
+/-! ### Families and classes -/
+
+/-- A family of width-`w` branching programs with accepting sets: `program n` takes `n` inputs and
+`accept n ⊆ ([w] → [w])` is its accepting set; the family recognizes
+`{u | (program |u|).yield u.get ∈ accept |u|}` [Bar89, §1, p001-b002: "`x ∈ L` iff
+`B(x) ∈ F_n`"].  Registered model (`TCSlib/ComputationalModels.lean`). -/
+structure BranchingProgramFamily (w : ℕ) where
+  /-- The program for inputs of length `n`. -/
+  program : (n : ℕ) → BranchingProgram w n
+  /-- The accepting set `F_n` for inputs of length `n`. -/
+  accept : (n : ℕ) → Finset (Fin w → Fin w)
+
+namespace BranchingProgramFamily
+
+variable {w : ℕ} (P : BranchingProgramFamily w)
+
+/-- The language a family recognizes:
+`P.language = {u | (P.program u.length).yield u.get ∈ P.accept u.length}`
+[Bar89, §1, p001-b002]. -/
+def language : Language Bool :=
+  {u | (P.program u.length).yield u.get ∈ P.accept u.length}
+
+/-- Reader of `language`:
+`u ∈ P.language ↔ (P.program u.length).yield u.get ∈ P.accept u.length`. -/
+theorem mem_language_iff (u : List Bool) :
+    u ∈ P.language ↔ (P.program u.length).yield u.get ∈ P.accept u.length :=
+  Iff.rfl
+
+/-- The language unfolded:
+`P.language = {u | (P.program u.length).yield u.get ∈ P.accept u.length}`. -/
+theorem language_eq :
+    P.language = {u | (P.program u.length).yield u.get ∈ P.accept u.length} := rfl
+
+/-- The family has polynomial length: `∃ a k, ∀ n, (P.program n).length ≤ a * (n + 1) ^ k`
+[Bar89, §1, p002-b001] (the `(n + 1)` form of `PPoly.lean`'s polynomial size). -/
+def IsPolyLength : Prop :=
+  ∃ a k : ℕ, ∀ n, (P.program n).length ≤ a * (n + 1) ^ k
+
+/-- Reader of `IsPolyLength`:
+`P.IsPolyLength ↔ ∃ a k, ∀ n, (P.program n).length ≤ a * (n + 1) ^ k`. -/
+theorem isPolyLength_iff :
+    P.IsPolyLength ↔ ∃ a k : ℕ, ∀ n, (P.program n).length ≤ a * (n + 1) ^ k :=
+  Iff.rfl
+
+/-- Every program of the family is a permutation program: `∀ n, (P.program n).IsPermutation`
+[Bar89, §1, p002-b001]. -/
+def IsPermutation : Prop :=
+  ∀ n, (P.program n).IsPermutation
+
+/-- Reader of `IsPermutation`: `P.IsPermutation ↔ ∀ n, (P.program n).IsPermutation`. -/
+theorem isPermutation_iff : P.IsPermutation ↔ ∀ n, (P.program n).IsPermutation :=
+  Iff.rfl
+
+end BranchingProgramFamily
+
+/-- `L ∈ BWBP`: for some width `w`, a family of width-`w` programs of polynomial length
+(`length ≤ a·(n+1)^k`) recognizes `L`,
+`∃ w (P : BranchingProgramFamily w), P.IsPolyLength ∧ P.language = L` [Bar89, §1, p002-b001]. -/
+def Language.InBWBP (L : Language Bool) : Prop :=
+  ∃ (w : ℕ) (P : BranchingProgramFamily w), P.IsPolyLength ∧ P.language = L
+
+/-- Reader of `Language.InBWBP`:
+`L.InBWBP ↔ ∃ w (P : BranchingProgramFamily w), P.IsPolyLength ∧ P.language = L`. -/
+theorem Language.inBWBP_iff (L : Language Bool) :
+    L.InBWBP ↔ ∃ (w : ℕ) (P : BranchingProgramFamily w), P.IsPolyLength ∧ P.language = L :=
+  Iff.rfl
+
+/-- `L ∈ PBP_w`: a family of width-`w` permutation programs of polynomial length
+(`length ≤ a·(n+1)^k`) recognizes `L`,
+`∃ P : BranchingProgramFamily w, P.IsPermutation ∧ P.IsPolyLength ∧ P.language = L`
+[Bar89, §1, p002-b001; Corollary, p004-b004: "polynomial-length 5-PBPs"]. -/
+def Language.InPBP (w : ℕ) (L : Language Bool) : Prop :=
+  ∃ P : BranchingProgramFamily w, P.IsPermutation ∧ P.IsPolyLength ∧ P.language = L
+
+/-- Reader of `Language.InPBP`:
+`L.InPBP w ↔ ∃ P : BranchingProgramFamily w, P.IsPermutation ∧ P.IsPolyLength ∧
+P.language = L`. -/
+theorem Language.inPBP_iff (w : ℕ) (L : Language Bool) :
+    L.InPBP w ↔
+      ∃ P : BranchingProgramFamily w, P.IsPermutation ∧ P.IsPolyLength ∧ P.language = L :=
+  Iff.rfl

+import Mathlib.Algebra.BigOperators.Group.Finset.Basic
+import Mathlib.Algebra.BigOperators.Group.Finset.Piecewise
+import Mathlib.Logic.Equiv.Basic
+import TCSlib.Complexity.BranchingPrograms.Defs

+/-!
+# The branching-program host `evalWith`
+
+A program run on a *state vector* `V : Fin w → α` instead of a single state: one instruction
+sends `V` to the vector whose coordinate `i` collects, through a selector `sel` applied to the
+instruction's input, the sums of the entries that the `0`-branch and the `1`-branch send to `i`
+(the push form).  Folding this step over the instructions in program order is the host
+`BranchingProgram.evalWith`, generic in the selector `sel : β → α → α → α` and in the value type
+`α` (an additive commutative monoid).  It is row H18 of the host registry
+(`machine-library-design.md` §14).  Its consumers are the Boolean semantics (C8: a one-hot state
+stays one-hot at the yield, for every program) and GSW's homomorphic evaluation of a permutation
+program, where `α` is a ciphertext type and `sel` the homomorphic selector.
+
+The push form needs no inverse, so the host is total and computable on the general model of
+[Bar89, §1]; for a permutation instruction each sum has exactly one term and the step is
+[BV14, Eq. (4)]'s pull form (C6).
+
+## Main definitions
+
+* `BranchingProgram.Instr.stepWith` — one instruction on a state vector (push form).
+* `BranchingProgram.evalWith` — the host: `stepWith` folded in program order.
+
+## Main results
+
+All are statements of the 6a statement phase (targets admitted with `sorry`), except the readers.
+
+* `BranchingProgram.evalWith_nil`, `evalWith_append` — readers of the fold (C5).
+* `BranchingProgram.Instr.stepWith_of_isPermutation` — BV14's pull form for a permutation
+  instruction (C6).
+* `BranchingProgram.evalWith_rel` — the step-indexed relational host lemma (C7).
+* `BranchingProgram.evalWith_single` — Boolean semantics for general maps (C8).
+
+## References
+
+* [Bar89] D. A. Mix Barrington, *Bounded-width polynomial-size branching programs recognize
+  exactly those languages in NC¹*, J. Comput. System Sci. 38(1):150–164, 1989.
+* [BV14] Z. Brakerski, V. Vaikuntanathan, *Lattice-based FHE as secure as PKE*, ITCS 2014,
+  pp. 1–12; IACR ePrint 2013/541.
+-/
+
+set_option maxHeartbeats 0
+set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
+set_option autoImplicit false
+
+namespace BranchingProgram
+
+open Finset
+
+variable {w n : ℕ} {α α' β β' : Type*}
+
+/-- One instruction on a state vector, push form: coordinate `i` of the new vector is
+`sel (inp ι.var) (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (ι.map0 · = i), V j) (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (ι.map1 · = i), V j)`,
+the selector applied to the instruction's input and to the sums of the entries that the
+`0`-branch and the `1`-branch send to `i`.  Host step; glue generalizing
+[BV14, Eq. (4), p013-b002] from permutations to general maps. -/
+def Instr.stepWith [AddCommMonoid α] (sel : β → α → α → α) (inp : Fin n → β) (ι : Instr w n)
+    (V : Fin w → α) : Fin w → α :=
+  fun i => sel (inp ι.var) (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (fun j => ι.map0 j = i), V j)
+    (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (fun j => ι.map1 j = i), V j)
+
+/-- Reader of `Instr.stepWith`: `ι.stepWith sel inp V i =
+sel (inp ι.var) (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (ι.map0 · = i), V j) (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (ι.map1 · = i), V j)`.
+-/
+theorem Instr.stepWith_apply [AddCommMonoid α] (sel : β → α → α → α) (inp : Fin n → β)
+    (ι : Instr w n) (V : Fin w → α) (i : Fin w) :
+    ι.stepWith sel inp V i = sel (inp ι.var) (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (fun j => ι.map0 j = i), V j)
+      (∑ j ∈ univ.filter (fun j => ι.map1 j = i), V j) := rfl
+
+/-- **The branching-program host** (registry row H18): fold `Instr.stepWith sel inp` over the
+instructions in program order, starting from `V`,
+`B.evalWith sel inp V = B.instrs.foldl (fun U ι => ι.stepWith sel inp U) V`.  Generic in the
+selector `sel` and the value type `α`; its consumers are the Boolean semantics (C8) and GSW's
+homomorphic evaluation (G2).  Glue generalizing [BV14, Eq. (4), p013-b002]. -/
+def evalWith [AddCommMonoid α] (sel : β → α → α → α) (inp : Fin n → β)
+    (B : BranchingProgram w n) (V : Fin w → α) : Fin w → α :=
+  B.instrs.foldl (fun U ι => ι.stepWith sel inp U) V
+
+/-- Reader of `evalWith`: `B.evalWith sel inp V = B.instrs.foldl (fun U ι => ι.stepWith sel inp U)
+V`. -/
+theorem evalWith_eq [AddCommMonoid α] (sel : β → α → α → α) (inp : Fin n → β)
+    (B : BranchingProgram w n) (V : Fin w → α) :
+    B.evalWith sel inp V = B.instrs.foldl (fun U ι => ι.stepWith sel inp U) V := rfl
+
+/-! ### Targets C5–C8 -/
+
+/-- **C5.** The empty program leaves the state unchanged:
+`(⟨[]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).evalWith sel inp V = V`.  Glue (reader of the fold).
+
+**Proof sketch.** `evalWith_eq` and `List.foldl_nil`. -/
+theorem evalWith_nil [AddCommMonoid α] (sel : β → α → α → α) (inp : Fin n → β)
+    (V : Fin w → α) : (⟨[]⟩ : BranchingProgram w n).evalWith sel inp V = V := by
+  rw [evalWith_eq, List.foldl_nil]
+
+/-- **C5.** The host runs a concatenation in program order:
+`(B₁ ++ B₂).evalWith sel inp V = B₂.evalWith sel inp (B₁.evalWith sel inp V)`.  Glue (reader of
+the fold).
+
+**Proof sketch.** `evalWith_eq`, the reader `append_instrs` of `++`, and `List.foldl_append`. -/
+theorem evalWith_append [AddCommMonoid α] (sel : β → α → α → α) (inp : Fin n → β)
+    (B₁ B₂ : BranchingProgram w n) (V : Fin w → α) :
+    (B₁ ++ B₂).evalWith sel inp V = B₂.evalWith sel inp (B₁.evalWith sel inp V) := by
+  rw [evalWith_eq, evalWith_eq sel inp B₂, evalWith_eq sel inp B₁, append_instrs,
+    List.foldl_append]
+
+/-- **C6.** For a permutation instruction each sum of the push form has exactly one term, and the
+step is BV14's pull form: `ι.stepWith sel inp V i = sel (inp ι.var) (V (σ₀⁻¹ i)) (V (σ₁⁻¹ i))`
+with `σ₀ = Equiv.ofBijective ι.map0 _`, `σ₁ = Equiv.ofBijective ι.map1 _`
+[BV14, Eq. (4), p013-b002: `v_t[i] := v_{t−1}[σ_{t,0}⁻¹(i)]·(1 − x) + v_{t−1}[σ_{t,1}⁻¹(i)]·x`].
+Deviation: BV14's selector is the arithmetic `(1 − x)·u + x·v`; here it is the abstract `sel`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Rewrite with `Instr.stepWith_apply`.  With `e = Equiv.ofBijective ι.map0 _`
+(bijective by `Instr.isPermutation_iff`), `ι.map0 j = i ↔ j = e.symm i`
+(`Equiv.apply_eq_iff_eq_symm_apply`), so the filter is the singleton `{e.symm i}`
+(`Finset.filter_eq'`) and the sum is `V (e.symm i)` (`Finset.sum_singleton`); the same for
+`map1`. -/
+theorem Instr.stepWith_of_isPermutation [AddCommMonoid α] (sel : β → α → α → α)
+    (inp : Fin n → β) {ι : Instr w n} (hι : ι.IsPermutation) (V : Fin w → α) (i : Fin w) :
+    ι.stepWith sel inp V i =
+      sel (inp ι.var) (V ((Equiv.ofBijective _ (ι.isPermutation_iff.1 hι).1).symm i))
+        (V ((Equiv.ofBijective _ (ι.isPermutation_iff.1 hι).2).symm i)) := by
+  -- for a bijection `f`, the push-form sum at `i` has the single term `j = f⁻¹ i`
+  have key : ∀ (f : Fin w → Fin w) (hf : Function.Bijective f),
+      ∑ j ∈ univ.filter (fun j => f j = i), V j = V ((Equiv.ofBijective f hf).symm i) := by
+    intro f hf
+    have hs : univ.filter (fun j => f j = i) = {(Equiv.ofBijective f hf).symm i} := by
+      ext j
+      rw [Finset.mem_filter, Finset.mem_singleton, ← Equiv.ofBijective_apply f hf,
+        Equiv.apply_eq_iff_eq_symm_apply]
+      exact ⟨fun h => h.2, fun h => ⟨Finset.mem_univ j, h⟩⟩
+    rw [hs, Finset.sum_singleton]
+  rw [Instr.stepWith_apply, key _ (ι.isPermutation_iff.1 hι).1,
+    key _ (ι.isPermutation_iff.1 hι).2]
+
+/-- **C7.** **Step-indexed relational host lemma**: two runs of the host on the same program,
+possibly with different selectors, inputs and value types, that are related by `R 0` at the
+start and whose relation is carried from `R t` to `R (t + 1)` by every instruction
+`B.instrs[t]`, end related by `R B.length`:
+`R 0 V V' → (∀ t < B.length, ∀ U U', R t U U' → R (t + 1) (ι_t.stepWith sel inp U)
+(ι_t.stepWith sel' inp' U')) → R B.length (B.evalWith sel inp V) (B.evalWith sel' inp' V')`.
+Glue; GSW carries its noise bound `t·2Nβ` in the index.
+
+**Proof sketch.** By `evalWith_eq` both sides are left folds over `B.instrs`.  Prove, by
+induction on `k ≤ B.instrs.length`, that the folds over `B.instrs.take k` are related by `R k`:
+the step `k → k + 1` uses `List.take_succ`, `List.foldl_append` and `hstep k` with
+`B.instrs[k]`; at `k = B.instrs.length` use `List.take_length` and `length_eq`. -/
+theorem evalWith_rel [AddCommMonoid α] [AddCommMonoid α'] (sel : β → α → α → α)
+    (sel' : β' → α' → α' → α') (inp : Fin n → β) (inp' : Fin n → β')
+    (B : BranchingProgram w n) (R : ℕ → (Fin w → α) → (Fin w → α') → Prop) (V : Fin w → α)
+    (V' : Fin w → α') (h0 : R 0 V V')
+    (hstep : ∀ t (ht : t < B.length) (U : Fin w → α) (U' : Fin w → α'), R t U U' →
+      R (t + 1) ((B.instrs[t]'(ht.trans_eq B.length_eq)).stepWith sel inp U)
+        ((B.instrs[t]'(ht.trans_eq B.length_eq)).stepWith sel' inp' U')) :
+    R B.length (B.evalWith sel inp V) (B.evalWith sel' inp' V') := by
+  -- the folds over the first `k` instructions are related by `R k`
+  have hrun : ∀ k (hk : k ≤ B.instrs.length),
+      R k ((B.instrs.take k).foldl (fun U ι => ι.stepWith sel inp U) V)
+        ((B.instrs.take k).foldl (fun U ι => ι.stepWith sel' inp' U) V') := by
+    intro k
+    induction k with
+    | zero => intro _; rw [List.take_zero, List.foldl_nil, List.foldl_nil]; exact h0
+    | succ k ih =>
+      intro hk
+      have hlt : k < B.instrs.length := hk
+      rw [List.take_succ_eq_append_getElem hlt, List.foldl_append, List.foldl_append,
+        List.foldl_cons, List.foldl_nil, List.foldl_cons, List.foldl_nil]
+      exact hstep k (B.length_eq ▸ hlt) _ _ (ih hlt.le)
+  have h := hrun B.instrs.length le_rfl
+  rw [List.take_length] at h
+  rw [evalWith_eq, evalWith_eq, length_eq]
+  exact h
+
+/-- One instruction of the Boolean host on a one-hot vector: with the selector
+`sel b u v = if b then v else u`, `ι.stepWith sel x (Pi.single s 1) = Pi.single (ι.eval x s) 1`.
+Glue for C8 [BV14, Eq. (4), p013-b002, generalized from permutations]; split on `x ι.var`. -/
+private theorem stepWith_single (ι : Instr w n) (x : Fin n → Bool) (s : Fin w) :
+    ι.stepWith (fun b (u v : ℕ) => if b then v else u) x (Pi.single s 1) =
+      Pi.single (ι.eval x s) 1 := by
+  funext i
+  -- each push-form sum of a one-hot vector is `Finset.sum_pi_single'`
+  rw [Instr.stepWith_apply, Finset.sum_pi_single', Finset.sum_pi_single', Pi.single_apply,
+    Instr.eval_eq]
+  simp only [Finset.mem_filter, Finset.mem_univ, true_and]
+  cases x ι.var <;> simp only [Bool.false_eq_true, ↓reduceIte] <;>
+    exact if_congr eq_comm rfl rfl
+
+/-- **C8.** Boolean semantics for **general** maps: with values in `ℕ` and the selector
+`sel b u v = if b then v else u`, the host started at the one-hot vector of `s` ends at the
+one-hot vector of `B.yield x s`:
+`B.evalWith (fun b u v => if b then v else u) x (Pi.single s 1) = Pi.single (B.yield x s) 1`
+[BV14, Eq. (4), p013-b002, generalized from permutations; Bar89, §1, p001-b002].
+Deviation: BV14 states Eq. (4) for permutations; the push form makes it hold for every map.
+
+**Proof sketch.** One step: for every instruction `ι`,
+`ι.stepWith sel x (Pi.single s 1) = Pi.single (ι.eval x s) 1`; at coordinate `i` the `0`-sum is
+`∑ j ∈ univ.filter (ι.map0 · = i), Pi.single s 1 j = if ι.map0 s = i then 1 else 0`
+(`Finset.sum_filter`, `Pi.single_apply`, `Finset.sum_ite_eq'`), likewise the `1`-sum, and the
+selector picks the branch of `x ι.var` as `Instr.eval_eq` does.  Then induct on `B.instrs` with
+the yield's fold generalized to a start map `f` (claim: the host fold from `Pi.single (f s) 1`
+is `Pi.single (fold f s) 1`), using `evalWith_eq` and `yield_eq`. -/
+theorem evalWith_single (B : BranchingProgram w n) (x : Fin n → Bool) (s : Fin w) :
+    B.evalWith (fun b (u v : ℕ) => if b then v else u) x (Pi.single s 1) =
+      Pi.single (B.yield x s) 1 := by
+  rw [evalWith_eq, yield_eq]
+  -- the induction of the sketch is core's `List.foldl_hom` for `f ↦ Pi.single (f s) 1`, whose
+  -- step is `stepWith_single`
+  exact List.foldl_hom (fun f : Fin w → Fin w => (Pi.single (f s) 1 : Fin w → ℕ)) (init := id)
+    (g₁ := fun f (ι : Instr w n) => ι.eval x ∘ f)
+    (g₂ := fun U ι => ι.stepWith (fun b (u v : ℕ) => if b then v else u) x U)
+    (fun f ι => stepWith_single ι x (f s))
+
+end BranchingProgram

+import TCSlib.Complexity.BranchingPrograms.Barrington

+/-!
+# Bounded-width branching programs and NC¹
+
+The class-level consequences of Barrington's two theorems [Bar89, §3]: the `DAGCircuit` form of
+Theorem 1 (length `≤ 4^depth`), NC¹ ⊆ polynomial-length width-5 permutation programs, BWBP ⊆ NC¹
+(Theorem 2 for families), and Barrington's Corollary BWBP = nonuniform NC¹ = PBP₅.  The classes
+`Language.InBWBP`, `Language.InPBP` are defined in `Defs.lean`; `Language.InNC` is the book's
+`DAGCircuit` class (`NCAC.lean`).  Both directions go through the formula class
+`Language.InTreeNC 1` (`Language.inNC_one_iff`).
+
+## Main definitions
+
+None: the classes are defined in `Defs.lean`, the constructions in `Barrington.lean` and
+`BarringtonConverse.lean`.
+
+## Main results
+
+All are statements of the 6a statement phase, proved in the fill phase (gsw-6a-fills).
+
+* `BranchingProgram.exists_fiveCycleRecognizes_of_dagCircuit` — Thm 1 for `DAGCircuit`s (C22).
+* `Language.InNC.inPBP_five` — NC¹ ⊆ PBP₅ (C23).
+* `Language.InBWBP.inNC_one` — BWBP ⊆ NC¹ (C24).
+* `Language.inNC_one_iff_inBWBP`, `Language.inNC_one_iff_inPBP_five` — the Corollary (C25).
+
+## Deviations
+
+* Inputs of length `0` have no variable for Barrington's constants to read (`[NeZero n]`); the
+  family statements handle `n = 0` through the accepting set (the empty program yields `id`, and
+  `F_0` is `{id}` or `∅` according to whether `[] ∈ L`).
+* The circuit of Theorem 2 is a formula (P6-Q18); it reaches `Language.InNC 1` through
+  `Language.InTreeNC.inNC`.
+
+## References
+
+* [Bar89] D. A. Mix Barrington, *Bounded-width polynomial-size branching programs recognize
+  exactly those languages in NC¹*, J. Comput. System Sci. 38(1):150–164, 1989.
+* [BV14] Z. Brakerski, V. Vaikuntanathan, *Lattice-based FHE as secure as PKE*, ITCS 2014,
+  pp. 1–12; IACR ePrint 2013/541.
+-/
+
+set_option maxHeartbeats 0
+set_option relaxedAutoImplicit false
+set_option autoImplicit false
+
+namespace BranchingProgram
+
+open BoolCircuit Equiv
+
+/-- **C22.** **The `DAGCircuit` corollary of Theorem 1**: a fan-in-two circuit `C` on `n ≥ 1`
+inputs is five-cycle recognized, with any five-cycle output `σ`, by a width-5 permutation program
+of length at most `4 ^ C.depth`:
+`∃ B, B.IsPermutation ∧ B.length ≤ 4 ^ C.depth ∧ FiveCycleRecognizes B (fun x => C.eval x = true) σ`
+[BV14, Thm 3.5, p014-b001; Bar89, Thm 1, p003-b002].
+Deviation: Bar89 and BV14 start from a circuit of AND/OR (BV14: NAND) gates; here the circuit is
+unfolded to a formula first (`DAGCircuit.toTree`, depth-preserving), P6-Q2.
+
+**Proof sketch.** Take `B = barrington C.toTree σ`.  From `hC` (`DAGCircuit.isFaninTwo_iff`, and
+the reader `DAGCircuit.isWellFormed_iff`) get
+well-formedness and fan-in `≤ 2` of every gate; then `toTree_maxFanin_le` gives
+`C.toTree.maxFanin ≤ 2`, `toTree_depth_le` gives `C.toTree.depth ≤ C.depth`, and `toTree_eval`
+gives `C.toTree.eval x = C.eval x`.  Conclude by `isPermutation_barrington`,
+`barrington_length_le` with `Nat.pow_le_pow_right`, and `barrington_recognizes`. -/
+theorem exists_fiveCycleRecognizes_of_dagCircuit {n : ℕ} [NeZero n] (C : DAGCircuit n)
+    (hC : C.IsFaninTwo) {σ : Perm (Fin 5)} (hσ : IsFiveCycle σ) :
+    ∃ B : BranchingProgram 5 n, B.IsPermutation ∧ B.length ≤ 4 ^ C.depth ∧
+      FiveCycleRecognizes B (fun x => C.eval x = true) σ := by
+  have hF := (DAGCircuit.isFaninTwo_iff C).1 hC
+  have hwf : C.IsWellFormed := (DAGCircuit.isWellFormed_iff C).2 hF.1
+  have hmf : C.toTree.maxFanin ≤ 2 := C.toTree_maxFanin_le hwf hF.2
+  refine ⟨barrington C.toTree σ, isPermutation_barrington C.toTree σ,
+    (barrington_length_le hmf σ).trans
+      (Nat.pow_le_pow_right (by norm_num) (C.toTree_depth_le hwf)), ?_⟩
+  have h := barrington_recognizes C.toTree hmf hσ
+  rw [fiveCycleRecognizes_iff] at h ⊢
+  refine ⟨h.1, fun x => ?_⟩
+  rw [← C.toTree_eval hwf x]
+  exact h.2 x
+
+end BranchingProgram
+
+open BoolCircuit BranchingProgram
+
+/-- **C23.** **NC¹ ⊆ polynomial-length width-5 permutation programs**: `L.InNC 1 → L.InPBP 5`
+[Bar89, Corollary, p004-b004; abstract, p001-b001].
+Deviation: inputs of length `0` are handled by the accepting set (Thm 1 needs `n ≥ 1`).
+
+**Proof sketch.** `Language.InNC.inTreeNC_one` gives a fan-in-2 formula family `C` with depth
+`≤ b·(log₂ n + 1)`, deciding `L` (read through the readers `Language.inTreeNC_iff`,
+`TreeCircuitFamily.hasFaninTwo_iff` and `hasPolylogDepth_iff`).
+Define the family with `program n = barrington (C.circuit n) sigma1` for `n ≥ 1` (`NeZero`
+from `n ≠ 0`) and `⟨[]⟩` for `n = 0`; `accept n = {sigma1}` for `n ≥ 1`, and `{id}` or `∅`
+at `n = 0` according to `[] ∈ L` (classical).  Permutation: `isPermutation_barrington`
+(`BranchingProgramFamily.isPermutation_iff`).
+Length: `barrington_length_le` and `4 ^ (b·(log₂ n + 1)) ≤ 4 ^ b · (n + 1) ^ (2b)`
+(`Nat.pow_le_pow_right`, `Nat.pow_log_le_add_one`), so `BranchingProgramFamily.isPolyLength_iff`.
+Language: `BranchingProgramFamily.mem_language_iff`, `barrington_recognizes` with
+`isFiveCycle_sigma1`, `TreeCircuitFamily.mem_language_iff`, and `⇑sigma1 ≠ id` (a five-cycle
+has `cycleType {5}`: `isFiveCycle_iff`, `Equiv.Perm.cycleType_one`); at `n = 0`, `yield_nil`.
+Conclude with `Language.inPBP_iff`. -/
+theorem Language.InNC.inPBP_five {L : Language Bool} (h : L.InNC 1) : L.InPBP 5 := by
+  classical
+  obtain ⟨C, hF, -, hD, hL⟩ := (Language.inTreeNC_iff 1 L).1 h.inTreeNC_one
+  obtain ⟨b, hb⟩ := (TreeCircuitFamily.hasPolylogDepth_iff C 1).1 hD
+  have hF' := (TreeCircuitFamily.hasFaninTwo_iff C).1 hF
+  -- the identity is not the five-cycle `sigma1`
+  have hσ1 : (⇑sigma1 : Fin 5 → Fin 5) ≠ id := by
+    intro h1
+    have h2 : sigma1 = 1 := Equiv.ext fun i => congrFun h1 i
+    have h3 := (isFiveCycle_iff sigma1).1 isFiveCycle_sigma1
+    rw [h2, Equiv.Perm.cycleType_one] at h3
+    exact Multiset.zero_ne_singleton 5 h3
+  refine (Language.inPBP_iff 5 L).2
+    ⟨⟨fun n => if h0 : n = 0 then ⟨[]⟩ else
+        haveI : NeZero n := ⟨h0⟩; barrington (C.circuit n) sigma1,
+      fun n => if n = 0 then (if ([] : List Bool) ∈ L then {id} else ∅) else {⇑sigma1}⟩,
+      ?_, ?_, ?_⟩
+  · -- permutation programs
+    refine (BranchingProgramFamily.isPermutation_iff _).2 fun n => ?_
+    dsimp only
+    by_cases h0 : n = 0
+    · rw [dif_pos h0, isPermutation_iff]
+      intro ι hι
+      exact absurd hι List.not_mem_nil
+    · rw [dif_neg h0]
+      haveI : NeZero n := ⟨h0⟩
+      exact isPermutation_barrington (C.circuit n) sigma1
+  · -- polynomial length: `4 ^ (b·(log₂ n + 1)) ≤ 4 ^ b · (n + 1) ^ (2b)`
+    refine (BranchingProgramFamily.isPolyLength_iff _).2 ⟨4 ^ b, 2 * b, fun n => ?_⟩
+    dsimp only
+    by_cases h0 : n = 0
+    · rw [dif_pos h0, length_eq]
+      exact Nat.zero_le _
+    · rw [dif_neg h0]
+      haveI : NeZero n := ⟨h0⟩
+      have hd : (C.circuit n).depth ≤ b * (Nat.log 2 n + 1) := by
+        simpa only [pow_one] using hb n
+      have h2 : (2 : ℕ) ^ Nat.log 2 n ≤ n + 1 := Nat.pow_log_le_add_one 2 n
+      calc (barrington (C.circuit n) sigma1).length ≤ 4 ^ (C.circuit n).depth :=
+            barrington_length_le (hF' n) sigma1
+        _ ≤ 4 ^ (b * (Nat.log 2 n + 1)) := Nat.pow_le_pow_right (by norm_num) hd
+        _ = 4 ^ b * ((2 : ℕ) ^ Nat.log 2 n) ^ (2 * b) := by
+            rw [show (4 : ℕ) = 2 ^ 2 by norm_num, ← pow_mul, ← pow_mul, ← pow_mul, ← pow_add]
+            ring
+        _ ≤ 4 ^ b * (n + 1) ^ (2 * b) :=
+            Nat.mul_le_mul_left _ (Nat.pow_le_pow_left h2 _)
+  · -- the language: `[]` through the accepting set, `n ≥ 1` through Theorem 1
+    ext u
+    rw [BranchingProgramFamily.mem_language_iff]
+    by_cases h0 : u.length = 0
+    · obtain rfl : u = [] := List.eq_nil_of_length_eq_zero h0
+      dsimp only
+      rw [dif_pos h0, if_pos h0, yield_nil]
+      by_cases hL0 : ([] : List Bool) ∈ L
+      · rw [if_pos hL0]
+        exact iff_of_true (Finset.mem_singleton_self _) hL0
+      · rw [if_neg hL0]
+        exact iff_of_false (Finset.notMem_empty _) hL0
+    · dsimp only
+      rw [dif_neg h0, if_neg h0, Finset.mem_singleton, ← hL, TreeCircuitFamily.mem_language_iff]
+      haveI : NeZero u.length := ⟨h0⟩
+      have hr := ((fiveCycleRecognizes_iff _ _ _).1
+        (barrington_recognizes (C.circuit u.length) (hF' u.length) isFiveCycle_sigma1)).2 u.get
+      by_cases he : (C.circuit u.length).eval u.get = true
+      · exact iff_of_true (by rw [hr.1 he]) he
+      · rw [hr.2 he]
+        exact iff_of_false (fun h1 => hσ1 h1.symm) he
+
+/-- **C24.** **BWBP ⊆ NC¹**, Theorem 2 for families: `L.InBWBP → L.InNC 1`
+[Bar89, Thm 2, p004-b003; abstract, p001-b001: "any bounded-width polynomial-size branching
+program can be simulated by an `NC¹` circuit"].
+Deviation: the circuits are fan-in-2 formulas (P6-Q18), compiled to `DAGCircuit`s by
+`Language.InTreeNC.inNC`.
+
+**Proof sketch.** From `Language.inBWBP_iff` get `w`, `P` and a length bound `a·(n+1)^k`
+(`BranchingProgramFamily.isPolyLength_iff`).  The formula family `n ↦ (P.program n).toFormula
+(P.accept n)` decides `P.language` (`toFormula_eval`, `BranchingProgramFamily.mem_language_iff`,
+`TreeCircuitFamily.mem_language_iff`), has fan-in 2 (`maxFanin_toFormula_le`), and depth
+`≤ (⌈log₂ w⌉ + 2)·⌈log₂ (a(n+1)^k)⌉ + topDepth w ≤ b·(log₂ n + 1)` with
+`b = (⌈log₂ w⌉ + 2)(a + k) + topDepth w` (`depth_toFormula_le`, `Nat.clog_mono_right`,
+`BoolCircuit.clog_poly_le`); its size is
+`≤ 2 ^ (depth + 1)` (`TreeCircuit.size_succ_le_two_pow`), hence polynomial
+(`Nat.pow_log_le_add_one`).  This gives
+`L.InTreeNC 1` (through the readers `Language.inTreeNC_iff`, `TreeCircuitFamily.hasFaninTwo_iff`,
+`isPolySize_iff` and `hasPolylogDepth_iff`), and `Language.InTreeNC.inNC` gives `L.InNC 1`. -/
+theorem Language.InBWBP.inNC_one {L : Language Bool} (h : L.InBWBP) : L.InNC 1 := by
+  obtain ⟨w, P, hP, hL⟩ := (Language.inBWBP_iff L).1 h
+  obtain ⟨a, k, ha⟩ := (BranchingProgramFamily.isPolyLength_iff P).1 hP
+  -- Step 1: the depth bound `b·(log₂ n + 1)` with `b = (⌈log₂ w⌉ + 2)(a + k) + topDepth w`
+  have hdepth : ∀ n, ((P.program n).toFormula (P.accept n)).depth ≤
+      ((Nat.clog 2 w + 2) * (a + k) + topDepth w) * (Nat.log 2 n + 1) := by
+    intro n
+    have hc : Nat.clog 2 (P.program n).length ≤ a + k * (Nat.log 2 n + 1) :=
+      (Nat.clog_mono_right 2 (ha n)).trans (BoolCircuit.clog_poly_le a k n)
+    have h1 : 1 ≤ Nat.log 2 n + 1 := Nat.le_add_left 1 _
+    calc ((P.program n).toFormula (P.accept n)).depth
+        ≤ (Nat.clog 2 w + 2) * Nat.clog 2 (P.program n).length + topDepth w :=
+          depth_toFormula_le _ _
+      _ ≤ (Nat.clog 2 w + 2) * (a + k * (Nat.log 2 n + 1)) + topDepth w :=
+          Nat.add_le_add_right (Nat.mul_le_mul_left _ hc) _
+      _ ≤ ((Nat.clog 2 w + 2) * (a + k) + topDepth w) * (Nat.log 2 n + 1) := by
+          have ha1 : a ≤ a * (Nat.log 2 n + 1) := Nat.le_mul_of_pos_right a h1
+          have ht1 : topDepth w ≤ topDepth w * (Nat.log 2 n + 1) :=
+            Nat.le_mul_of_pos_right _ h1
+          have hm := Nat.mul_le_mul_left (Nat.clog 2 w + 2)
+            (Nat.add_le_add_right ha1 (k * (Nat.log 2 n + 1)))
+          calc (Nat.clog 2 w + 2) * (a + k * (Nat.log 2 n + 1)) + topDepth w
+              ≤ (Nat.clog 2 w + 2) * (a * (Nat.log 2 n + 1) + k * (Nat.log 2 n + 1)) +
+                  topDepth w * (Nat.log 2 n + 1) := Nat.add_le_add hm ht1
+            _ = ((Nat.clog 2 w + 2) * (a + k) + topDepth w) * (Nat.log 2 n + 1) := by ring
+  -- Step 2: the formula family is in `NC¹` (fan-in, size `≤ 2 ^ (depth + 1)`, depth, language)
+  refine Language.InTreeNC.inNC ((Language.inTreeNC_iff 1 L).2
+    ⟨⟨fun n => (P.program n).toFormula (P.accept n)⟩, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩)
+  · exact (TreeCircuitFamily.hasFaninTwo_iff _).2 fun n => maxFanin_toFormula_le _ _
+  · refine (TreeCircuitFamily.isPolySize_iff _).2
+      ⟨2 ^ ((Nat.clog 2 w + 2) * (a + k) + topDepth w + 1),
+        (Nat.clog 2 w + 2) * (a + k) + topDepth w, fun n => ?_⟩
+    have hs := TreeCircuit.size_succ_le_two_pow _ (maxFanin_toFormula_le (P.program n) (P.accept n))
+    have h2 : (2 : ℕ) ^ Nat.log 2 n ≤ n + 1 := Nat.pow_log_le_add_one 2 n
+    set B := (Nat.clog 2 w + 2) * (a + k) + topDepth w
+    calc ((P.program n).toFormula (P.accept n)).size
+        ≤ 2 ^ (((P.program n).toFormula (P.accept n)).depth + 1) := by omega
+      _ ≤ 2 ^ (B * (Nat.log 2 n + 1) + 1) := Nat.pow_le_pow_right (by norm_num)
+          (Nat.add_le_add_right (hdepth n) 1)
+      _ = 2 ^ (B + 1) * ((2 : ℕ) ^ Nat.log 2 n) ^ B := by
+          rw [← pow_mul, ← pow_add]; ring_nf
+      _ ≤ 2 ^ (B + 1) * (n + 1) ^ B := Nat.mul_le_mul_left _ (Nat.pow_le_pow_left h2 _)
+  · exact (TreeCircuitFamily.hasPolylogDepth_iff _ 1).2
+      ⟨_, fun n => by rw [pow_one]; exact hdepth n⟩
+  · ext u
+    rw [TreeCircuitFamily.mem_language_iff, ← hL, BranchingProgramFamily.mem_language_iff]
+    dsimp only
+    rw [toFormula_eval, decide_eq_true_iff]
+
+/-- **C25.** **Barrington's Corollary**, first equality: nonuniform NC¹ is BWBP,
+`L.InNC 1 ↔ L.InBWBP` [Bar89, Corollary, p004-b004].
+
+**Proof sketch.** `→`: `Language.InNC.inPBP_five`, then forget permutation-ness and take `w = 5`
+(`Language.inPBP_iff`, `Language.inBWBP_iff`).  `←`: `Language.InBWBP.inNC_one`. -/
+theorem Language.inNC_one_iff_inBWBP (L : Language Bool) : L.InNC 1 ↔ L.InBWBP := by
+  refine ⟨fun h => ?_, fun h => h.inNC_one⟩
+  obtain ⟨P, -, hP, hL⟩ := (Language.inPBP_iff 5 L).1 h.inPBP_five
+  exact (Language.inBWBP_iff L).2 ⟨5, P, hP, hL⟩
+
+/-- **C25.** **Barrington's Corollary**, second equality: nonuniform NC¹ is the class of
+polynomial-length width-5 permutation programs, `L.InNC 1 ↔ L.InPBP 5`
+[Bar89, Corollary, p004-b004].
+
+**Proof sketch.** `→`: `Language.InNC.inPBP_five`.  `←`: a `PBP₅` family is a BWBP family
+(`Language.inPBP_iff`, `Language.inBWBP_iff`), then `Language.InBWBP.inNC_one`. -/
+theorem Language.inNC_one_iff_inPBP_five (L : Language Bool) : L.InNC 1 ↔ L.InPBP 5 := by
+  refine ⟨fun h => h.inPBP_five, fun h => ?_⟩
+  obtain ⟨P, -, hP, hL⟩ := (Language.inPBP_iff 5 L).1 h
+  exact ((Language.inBWBP_iff L).2 ⟨5, P, hP, hL⟩).inNC_one

 import TCSlib.Complexity.CircuitComplexity.TreeDAG
 import TCSlib.Complexity.CircuitComplexity.DAGTransform
 import TCSlib.Complexity.CircuitComplexity.DAGFanin
+import TCSlib.Complexity.CircuitComplexity.NANDForm
+import TCSlib.Complexity.CircuitComplexity.NANDLayering

+# Formula arithmetic
+
+Shared combinators and arithmetic on `BoolCircuit.TreeCircuit` formulas: the circuit and formula
+gadget layer of `machine-library-design.md` ("Circuit and formula gadgets").  New formulas are
+assembled from it rather than built by hand.
+
+## Contents
+

+  two, `⌈log₂ |cs|⌉ + 1` extra depth), with its value, fan-in, depth and size lemmas; De Morgan
+  negation `neg`, substitution `subst`, `const`, and the gadgets `and2`, `or2`, `xor2`,
+  `xor3`, `maj3`.

+- `FormulaArith.Compare`: the carry-out `carryOut` and the comparison with a constant `geConst`.
+- `FormulaArith.ModSum`: the modular interval sum `modSumInterval` and its depth `modSumDepth`.

+/-
+Copyright (c) 2026 TCSlib contributors. All rights reserved.
